========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m here because I feel like I’m kind of falling apart, honestly. Ever since the foreclosure notices started coming in, I haven’t been myself at all. I’ll be sitting in my truck before work, just crying, trying to pull it together before I go into the bay, and then the phone rings and I practically jump out of my skin because I’m scared it’s more bad news.

And at work—I’m a mechanic, I work on heavy equipment, so you really can’t be distracted—but I’ve been snapping at the apprentices over little things, and I’ve been making these stupid mistakes I never would’ve made before. Like things I know better than to miss. It’s embarrassing, and it scares me, because I’ve always been the person who keeps it together. Lately it feels like I’m coming apart at the seams.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really, no. I mean… I used to be pretty good about having things outside of work, you know? Church volunteering, my fitness classes, and I’m in this truck club where we’d do outings and meetups, and normally I’d make time for all of that even when I was tired.

But lately I’ve just stopped showing up. I’ll tell myself I’m going to go, and then when it’s time, I just… don’t. It feels like too much, or I feel embarrassed because I don’t want people asking how I’m doing and then I have to pretend I’m fine. So I’ve kind of disappeared from all those things.

And work has changed too. I got taken out of the high-risk repair bay, which… that one really stings, because that’s what I’m good at. Or was good at, I guess. I know it’s probably for safety, but it makes me feel like I’m not even trusted to do my own job right now. So, no, I’m not really enjoying much of anything I normally would.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not much, honestly. I keep thinking if I just push myself to do the normal things, maybe I’ll feel like myself again, but I don’t really get that lift from them anymore.

Like with church volunteering—I used to feel useful there, like I was part of something steady. And my fitness classes, even when I complained about going, I always felt better afterward. The truck-club outings too, those were fun for me, getting out and talking with people who understood that whole world. But lately I’ve just stopped showing up. I’ll make excuses or just not answer messages because the thought of going feels like another job I can’t handle.

And at work, being taken out of the high-risk repair bay… that’s been really hard. That was the part of the job where I felt competent, where I knew I was good. Now even work doesn’t give me that same sense of pride. It’s like the things that used to make me feel like me are still there, but I can’t really connect to them.

I’ll be sitting in my truck before work, just crying, trying to pull it together before I go into the bay, and then the phone rings and I practically jump out of my skin because I’m scared it’s more bad news.\n\nAnd at work—I’m a mechanic, I work on heavy equipment, so you really can’t be distracted—but I’ve been snapping at the apprentices over little things, and I’ve been making these stupid mistakes I never would’ve made before. Like things I know better than to miss. It’s embarrassing, and it scares me, because I’ve always been the person who keeps it together. Lately it feels like I’m coming apart at the seams.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I’ll tell myself I’m going to go, and then when it’s time, I just… don’t. It feels like too much, or I feel embarrassed because I don’t want people asking how I’m doing and then I have to pretend I’m fine. So I’ve kind of disappeared from all those things.\n\nAnd work has changed too. I got taken out of the high-risk repair bay, which… that one really stings, because that’s what I’m good at. Or was good at, I guess. I know it’s probably for safety, but it makes me feel like I’m not even trusted to do my own job right now. So, no, I’m not really enjoying much of anything I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Are you still able to find pleasure in the things you do?\n\nPatient: Not much, honestly. I keep thinking if I just push myself to do the normal things, maybe I’ll feel like myself again, but I don’t really get that lift from them anymore.\n\nLike with church volunteering—I used to feel useful there, like I was part of something steady. And my fitness classes, even when I complained about going, I always felt better afterward. The truck-club outings too, those were fun for me, getting out and talking with people who understood that whole world. But lately I’ve just stopped showing up. I’ll make excuses or just not answer messages because the thought of going feels like another job I can’t handle.\n\nAnd at work, being taken out of the high-risk repair bay… that’s been really hard. That was the part of the job where I felt competent, where I knew I was good. Now even work doesn’t give me that same sense of pride. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I… I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I don’t think I’ve been sitting there making a plan to hurt myself, if that’s what you mean. It’s more like… I get overwhelmed and I don’t want to feel the way I’m feeling, and sometimes I just want everything to stop for a while.

I’m not trying to be evasive. It’s just hard to separate out what counts as “hurting myself” versus being so tired and ashamed and stressed that I don’t know what to do with myself. I haven’t really talked about it much because it sounds dramatic when I say it out loud, and I don’t want people to overreact, but I also don’t want to pretend everything’s fine.

And my fitness classes, even when I complained about going, I always felt better afterward. The truck-club outings too, those were fun for me, getting out and talking with people who understood that whole world. But lately I’ve just stopped showing up. I’ll make excuses or just not answer messages because the thought of going feels like another job I can’t handle.\n\nAnd at work, being taken out of the high-risk repair bay… that’s been really hard. That was the part of the job where I felt competent, where I knew I was good. Now even work doesn’t give me that same sense of pride. It’s like the things that used to make me feel like me are still there, but I can’t really connect to them.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. It’s just hard to separate out what counts as “hurting myself” versus being so tired and ashamed and stressed that I don’t know what to do with myself. I’m… not sure. I mean, I’ve had moments where I think it would be easier if I didn’t have to keep dealing with all of this, or if I could just disappear for a while and not have everyone needing an answer from me. So… I guess I don’t know. Maybe sometimes it gets close to that thought, but I don’t have a clear answer.

I haven’t really talked about it much because it sounds dramatic when I say it out loud, and I don’t want people to overreact, but I also don’t want to pretend everything’s fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought that you would be better off dead?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure. But I also don’t want to make it sound like I’m sitting around clearly thinking, “I should be dead,” because that’s not how it feels in my head. It’s more muddled than that. Like I’m exhausted and ashamed and I just want out from under it for a minute.\n\nSo… I guess I don’t know. It’s been about four months now, I think. It kind of started when the foreclosure notices began coming in. At first I told myself I was just stressed and that once I got through the next letter or phone call or whatever, I’d settle back down. But it didn’t really lift.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because I feel like I’m kind of falling apart, honestly. Ever since the foreclosure notices started coming in, I haven’t been myself at all. I’ll be sitting in my truck before work, just crying, trying to pull it together before I go into the bay, and then the phone rings and I practically jump out of my skin because I’m scared it’s more bad news.\n\nAnd at work—I’m a mechanic, I work on heavy equipment, so you really can’t be distracted—but I’ve been snapping at the apprentices over little things, and I’ve been making these stupid mistakes I never would’ve made before. Like things I know better than to miss. It’s embarrassing, and it scares me, because I’ve always been the person who keeps it together. Lately it feels like I’m coming apart at the seams.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I know my sleep hasn’t felt normal, but I haven’t really been keeping track of it or anything. Some nights I’m just lying there thinking too much, and then other nights I’m so worn out I don’t even know when I fell asleep. It’s hard for me to tell what counts as “trouble sleeping” versus just being exhausted and stressed, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I used to be pretty good about having things outside of work, you know? Church volunteering, my fitness classes, and I’m in this truck club where we’d do outings and meetups, and normally I’d make time for all of that even when I was tired.\n\nBut lately I’ve just stopped showing up. I’ll tell myself I’m going to go, and then when it’s time, I just… don’t. It feels like too much, or I feel embarrassed because I don’t want people asking how I’m doing and then I have to pretend I’m fine. So I’ve kind of disappeared from all those things.\n\nAnd work has changed too. I got taken out of the high-risk repair bay, which… that one really stings, because that’s what I’m good at. Or was good at, I guess. I know it’s probably for safety, but it makes me feel like I’m not even trusted to do my own job right now. So, no, I’m not really enjoying much of anything I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Are you still able to find pleasure in the things you do?\n\nPatient: Not much, honestly. I keep thinking if I just push myself to do the normal things, maybe I’ll feel like myself again, but I don’t really get that lift from them anymore.\n\nLike with church volunteering—I used to feel useful there, like I was part of something steady. I don’t think I’ve been sitting there making a plan to hurt myself, if that’s what you mean. Since then it’s just been hanging over me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I mean, I know my sleep hasn’t felt normal, but I haven’t really been keeping track of it or anything. I mean… I used to be pretty good about having things outside of work, you know? I’ll tell myself I’m going to go, and then when it’s time, I just… don’t. I haven’t been tracking it or getting on a scale much. My clothes maybe feel a little different some days, but I don’t know if that’s actual weight change or just me not paying much attention to myself lately.\n\nI wouldn’t want to say I’ve lost or gained a significant amount because I don’t really have a number for you. If it’s happened, it hasn’t been obvious enough that I could point to it and say, yes, that’s definitely changed.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you experienced any periods in the last four months where you felt somewhat better, even if only for a few days?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 47 / heavy_vehicle_or_mobile_equipment_service_technician_or_mechanic / some_college\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.   },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I’d call it “better,” exactly. There have been a few days where I functioned a little more normally, I guess—like I got through the day and wasn’t actively thinking about it every second. But even then it’s kind of like background noise, if that makes sense. It’s still there, just not as loud for a little while.

I can’t think of a stretch where I felt like myself again for several days in a row. Maybe part of a day here or there, or a day where I was distracted enough that I seemed okay on the outside. But then something would remind me, or I’d have a quiet moment, and it would all come right back.

So… somewhat better, maybe in small patches. Not really better in a way that lasted.

I mean, I know my sleep hasn’t felt normal, but I haven’t really been keeping track of it or anything. Some nights I’m just lying there thinking too much, and then other nights I’m so worn out I don’t even know when I fell asleep. It’s hard for me to tell what counts as “trouble sleeping” versus just being exhausted and stressed, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I used to be pretty good about having things outside of work, you know? I’ll tell myself I’m going to go, and then when it’s time, I just… don’t. It feels like too much, or I feel embarrassed because I don’t want people asking how I’m doing and then I have to pretend I’m fine. And my fitness classes, even when I complained about going, I always felt better afterward. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.   },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not sure I’d say “pleasure” exactly. There are things I still do, but it feels more like I’m going through the motions than actually enjoying them.

Like, sometimes I’ll put on a show or tinker with something around the house, and for a little bit I’m occupied, but I don’t get that same feeling of, I don’t know, being pulled into it or looking forward to it. It’s more like I’m trying to keep my hands busy so I don’t just sit there.

I guess there are moments where something is mildly distracting or comforting, but I can’t think of anything lately where I’d say, yes, that actually made me happy the way it used to. It’s hard to tell if I’m not enjoying things at all, or if I’m just too preoccupied to notice.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because I feel like I’m kind of falling apart, honestly. Ever since the foreclosure notices started coming in, I haven’t been myself at all. I’ll be sitting in my truck before work, just crying, trying to pull it together before I go into the bay, and then the phone rings and I practically jump out of my skin because I’m scared it’s more bad news.\n\nAnd at work—I’m a mechanic, I work on heavy equipment, so you really can’t be distracted—but I’ve been snapping at the apprentices over little things, and I’ve been making these stupid mistakes I never would’ve made before. Like things I know better than to miss. It’s embarrassing, and it scares me, because I’ve always been the person who keeps it together. Lately it feels like I’m coming apart at the seams.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I know my sleep hasn’t felt normal, but I haven’t really been keeping track of it or anything. Some nights I’m just lying there thinking too much, and then other nights I’m so worn out I don’t even know when I fell asleep. It’s hard for me to tell what counts as “trouble sleeping” versus just being exhausted and stressed, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I used to be pretty good about having things outside of work, you know? Church volunteering, my fitness classes, and I’m in this truck club where we’d do outings and meetups, and normally I’d make time for all of that even when I was tired.\n\nBut lately I’ve just stopped showing up. I’ll tell myself I’m going to go, and then when it’s time, I just… don’t. It feels like too much, or I feel embarrassed because I don’t want people asking how I’m doing and then I have to pretend I’m fine. So I’ve kind of disappeared from all those things.\n\nAnd work has changed too. I got taken out of the high-risk repair bay, which… that one really stings, because that’s what I’m good at. Or was good at, I guess. I know it’s probably for safety, but it makes me feel like I’m not even trusted to do my own job right now. So, no, I’m not really enjoying much of anything I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Are you still able to find pleasure in the things you do?\n\nPatient: Not much, honestly. I keep thinking if I just push myself to do the normal things, maybe I’ll feel like myself again, but I don’t really get that lift from them anymore.\n\nLike with church volunteering—I used to feel useful there, like I was part of something steady. And my fitness classes, even when I complained about going, I always felt better afterward. The truck-club outings too, those were fun for me, getting out and talking with people who understood that whole world. But lately I’ve just stopped showing up. I’ll make excuses or just not answer messages because the thought of going feels like another job I can’t handle.\n\nAnd at work, being taken out of the high-risk repair bay… that’s been really hard. That was the part of the job where I felt competent, where I knew I was good. Now even work doesn’t give me that same sense of pride. It’s like the things that used to make me feel like me are still there, but I can’t really connect to them.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I don’t think I’ve been sitting there making a plan to hurt myself, if that’s what you mean. It’s more like… I get overwhelmed and I don’t want to feel the way I’m feeling, and sometimes I just want everything to stop for a while.\n\nI’m not trying to be evasive. It’s just hard to separate out what counts as “hurting myself” versus being so tired and ashamed and stressed that I don’t know what to do with myself. I haven’t really talked about it much because it sounds dramatic when I say it out loud, and I don’t want people to overreact, but I also don’t want to pretend everything’s fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought that you would be better off dead?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure. I mean, I’ve had moments where I think it would be easier if I didn’t have to keep dealing with all of this, or if I could just disappear for a while and not have everyone needing an answer from me. But “better off dead” sounds so final, and I don’t know if that’s exactly what I mean.\n\nI don’t want to say no too quickly, because then it sounds like I’m brushing it off, and I don’t want to lie to you. But I also don’t want to make it sound like I’m sitting around clearly thinking, “I should be dead,” because that’s not how it feels in my head. It’s more muddled than that. Like I’m exhausted and ashamed and I just want out from under it for a minute.\n\nSo… I guess I don’t know. Maybe sometimes it gets close to that thought, but I don’t have a clear answer.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been about four months now, I think. It kind of started when the foreclosure notices began coming in. At first I told myself I was just stressed and that once I got through the next letter or phone call or whatever, I’d settle back down. But it didn’t really lift.\n\nSo… yeah, roughly four months. It’s hard to put an exact day on it, because it crept up on me, but those notices were when I remember feeling like something really shifted. Since then it’s just been hanging over me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss or gain since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I haven’t been tracking it or getting on a scale much. My clothes maybe feel a little different some days, but I don’t know if that’s actual weight change or just me not paying much attention to myself lately.\n\nI wouldn’t want to say I’ve lost or gained a significant amount because I don’t really have a number for you. If it’s happened, it hasn’t been obvious enough that I could point to it and say, yes, that’s definitely changed.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods in the last four months where you felt somewhat better, even if only for a few days?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it “better,” exactly. There have been a few days where I functioned a little more normally, I guess—like I got through the day and wasn’t actively thinking about it every second. But even then it’s kind of like background noise, if that makes sense. It’s still there, just not as loud for a little while.\n\nI can’t think of a stretch where I felt like myself again for several days in a row. Maybe part of a day here or there, or a day where I was distracted enough that I seemed okay on the outside. But then something would remind me, or I’d have a quiet moment, and it would all come right back.\n\nSo… somewhat better, maybe in small patches. Not really better in a way that lasted.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “pleasure” exactly. There are things I still do, but it feels more like I’m going through the motions than actually enjoying them.\n\nLike, sometimes I’ll put on a show or tinker with something around the house, and for a little bit I’m occupied, but I don’t get that same feeling of, I don’t know, being pulled into it or looking forward to it. It’s more like I’m trying to keep my hands busy so I don’t just sit there.\n\nI guess there are moments where something is mildly distracting or comforting, but I can’t think of anything lately where I’d say, yes, that actually made me happy the way it used to. It’s hard to tell if I’m not enjoying things at all, or if I’m just too preoccupied to notice.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you had any days in the past week where you felt no sadness at all?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 47 / heavy_vehicle_or_mobile_equipment_service_technician_or_mechanic / some_college\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response\nmanifestation: \"Ever since those foreclosure notices started coming, I feel like I’m coming apart at the seams—crying in my truck before work, jumping every time the phone rings, snapping at the apprentices, and making stupid mistakes in the repair bay that I never would’ve made before.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No… I don’t think I’ve had a whole day with no sadness at all. Maybe there are minutes where I’m distracted, like if I’m focused on a repair or just trying to get through something, but it’s still kind of sitting there underneath everything.

Ever since those foreclosure notices started coming, I just feel like I’m coming apart at the seams. I’ll be in my truck before work crying and trying to pull myself together so nobody sees it. Then the phone rings and I jump, because I’m scared it’s going to be more bad news. Even at work, where I’m usually steady, I’ve been snapping at the apprentices and making stupid mistakes in the repair bay that I would never normally make.

So no, not a day with no sadness. It may get pushed to the side for a little while, but it doesn’t really leave.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because I feel like I’m kind of falling apart, honestly. Ever since the foreclosure notices started coming in, I haven’t been myself at all. I’ll be sitting in my truck before work, just crying, trying to pull it together before I go into the bay, and then the phone rings and I practically jump out of my skin because I’m scared it’s more bad news.\n\nAnd at work—I’m a mechanic, I work on heavy equipment, so you really can’t be distracted—but I’ve been snapping at the apprentices over little things, and I’ve been making these stupid mistakes I never would’ve made before. Like things I know better than to miss. It’s embarrassing, and it scares me, because I’ve always been the person who keeps it together. Lately it feels like I’m coming apart at the seams.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I know my sleep hasn’t felt normal, but I haven’t really been keeping track of it or anything. Some nights I’m just lying there thinking too much, and then other nights I’m so worn out I don’t even know when I fell asleep. It’s hard for me to tell what counts as “trouble sleeping” versus just being exhausted and stressed, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I used to be pretty good about having things outside of work, you know? Church volunteering, my fitness classes, and I’m in this truck club where we’d do outings and meetups, and normally I’d make time for all of that even when I was tired.\n\nBut lately I’ve just stopped showing up. I’ll tell myself I’m going to go, and then when it’s time, I just… don’t. It feels like too much, or I feel embarrassed because I don’t want people asking how I’m doing and then I have to pretend I’m fine. So I’ve kind of disappeared from all those things.\n\nAnd work has changed too. I got taken out of the high-risk repair bay, which… that one really stings, because that’s what I’m good at. Or was good at, I guess. I know it’s probably for safety, but it makes me feel like I’m not even trusted to do my own job right now. So, no, I’m not really enjoying much of anything I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Are you still able to find pleasure in the things you do?\n\nPatient: Not much, honestly. I keep thinking if I just push myself to do the normal things, maybe I’ll feel like myself again, but I don’t really get that lift from them anymore.\n\nLike with church volunteering—I used to feel useful there, like I was part of something steady. And my fitness classes, even when I complained about going, I always felt better afterward. The truck-club outings too, those were fun for me, getting out and talking with people who understood that whole world. But lately I’ve just stopped showing up. I’ll make excuses or just not answer messages because the thought of going feels like another job I can’t handle.\n\nAnd at work, being taken out of the high-risk repair bay… that’s been really hard. That was the part of the job where I felt competent, where I knew I was good. Now even work doesn’t give me that same sense of pride. It’s like the things that used to make me feel like me are still there, but I can’t really connect to them.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I don’t think I’ve been sitting there making a plan to hurt myself, if that’s what you mean. It’s more like… I get overwhelmed and I don’t want to feel the way I’m feeling, and sometimes I just want everything to stop for a while.\n\nI’m not trying to be evasive. It’s just hard to separate out what counts as “hurting myself” versus being so tired and ashamed and stressed that I don’t know what to do with myself. I haven’t really talked about it much because it sounds dramatic when I say it out loud, and I don’t want people to overreact, but I also don’t want to pretend everything’s fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought that you would be better off dead?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure. I mean, I’ve had moments where I think it would be easier if I didn’t have to keep dealing with all of this, or if I could just disappear for a while and not have everyone needing an answer from me. But “better off dead” sounds so final, and I don’t know if that’s exactly what I mean.\n\nI don’t want to say no too quickly, because then it sounds like I’m brushing it off, and I don’t want to lie to you. But I also don’t want to make it sound like I’m sitting around clearly thinking, “I should be dead,” because that’s not how it feels in my head. It’s more muddled than that. Like I’m exhausted and ashamed and I just want out from under it for a minute.\n\nSo… I guess I don’t know. Maybe sometimes it gets close to that thought, but I don’t have a clear answer.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been about four months now, I think. It kind of started when the foreclosure notices began coming in. At first I told myself I was just stressed and that once I got through the next letter or phone call or whatever, I’d settle back down. But it didn’t really lift.\n\nSo… yeah, roughly four months. It’s hard to put an exact day on it, because it crept up on me, but those notices were when I remember feeling like something really shifted. Since then it’s just been hanging over me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss or gain since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I haven’t been tracking it or getting on a scale much. My clothes maybe feel a little different some days, but I don’t know if that’s actual weight change or just me not paying much attention to myself lately.\n\nI wouldn’t want to say I’ve lost or gained a significant amount because I don’t really have a number for you. If it’s happened, it hasn’t been obvious enough that I could point to it and say, yes, that’s definitely changed.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods in the last four months where you felt somewhat better, even if only for a few days?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it “better,” exactly. There have been a few days where I functioned a little more normally, I guess—like I got through the day and wasn’t actively thinking about it every second. But even then it’s kind of like background noise, if that makes sense. It’s still there, just not as loud for a little while.\n\nI can’t think of a stretch where I felt like myself again for several days in a row. No, I really don’t think so. Not completely. This has been going on for about four months now, pretty much since the foreclosure notices started coming in, and I can’t point to any time in there where I felt like, “Okay, I’m back to me.”

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… there are several, honestly. I used to be a lot more active with things outside of work, and now I just can’t seem to get anything out of them, or I don’t even make myself go.

Church volunteering was a big one. I used to show up pretty regularly and help out, and it made me feel useful, like I was connected to people. Lately I’ve just stopped going. Same with my fitness classes — I used to feel better afterward, even if I had to drag myself there, but now the idea of being around everyone and going through the routine just feels pointless.

And I used to go to truck-club outings, too. That was something I actually looked forward to before, talking shop and seeing what people were working on. Now I either don’t go, or if I think about going, it feels like I’d just be standing there pretending.

Even at work it’s affected things. I’ve been taken out of the high-risk repair bay, which… that’s hard to admit, because I used to handle that kind of work. But I’m not where I need to be, and they noticed. So yes, there are definitely things I used to enjoy or take pride in that I just don’t feel able to take pleasure in right now.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because I feel like I’m kind of falling apart, honestly. Ever since the foreclosure notices started coming in, I haven’t been myself at all. I’ll be sitting in my truck before work, just crying, trying to pull it together before I go into the bay, and then the phone rings and I practically jump out of my skin because I’m scared it’s more bad news.\n\nAnd at work—I’m a mechanic, I work on heavy equipment, so you really can’t be distracted—but I’ve been snapping at the apprentices over little things, and I’ve been making these stupid mistakes I never would’ve made before. Like things I know better than to miss. It’s embarrassing, and it scares me, because I’ve always been the person who keeps it together. Lately it feels like I’m coming apart at the seams.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I know my sleep hasn’t felt normal, but I haven’t really been keeping track of it or anything. Some nights I’m just lying there thinking too much, and then other nights I’m so worn out I don’t even know when I fell asleep. It’s hard for me to tell what counts as “trouble sleeping” versus just being exhausted and stressed, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I used to be pretty good about having things outside of work, you know? Church volunteering, my fitness classes, and I’m in this truck club where we’d do outings and meetups, and normally I’d make time for all of that even when I was tired.\n\nBut lately I’ve just stopped showing up. I’ll tell myself I’m going to go, and then when it’s time, I just… don’t. It feels like too much, or I feel embarrassed because I don’t want people asking how I’m doing and then I have to pretend I’m fine. So I’ve kind of disappeared from all those things.\n\nAnd work has changed too. I got taken out of the high-risk repair bay, which… that one really stings, because that’s what I’m good at. Or was good at, I guess. I know it’s probably for safety, but it makes me feel like I’m not even trusted to do my own job right now. So, no, I’m not really enjoying much of anything I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Are you still able to find pleasure in the things you do?\n\nPatient: Not much, honestly. I keep thinking if I just push myself to do the normal things, maybe I’ll feel like myself again, but I don’t really get that lift from them anymore.\n\nLike with church volunteering—I used to feel useful there, like I was part of something steady. And my fitness classes, even when I complained about going, I always felt better afterward. The truck-club outings too, those were fun for me, getting out and talking with people who understood that whole world. But lately I’ve just stopped showing up. I’ll make excuses or just not answer messages because the thought of going feels like another job I can’t handle.\n\nAnd at work, being taken out of the high-risk repair bay… that’s been really hard. That was the part of the job where I felt competent, where I knew I was good. Now even work doesn’t give me that same sense of pride. It’s like the things that used to make me feel like me are still there, but I can’t really connect to them.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I don’t think I’ve been sitting there making a plan to hurt myself, if that’s what you mean. It’s more like… I get overwhelmed and I don’t want to feel the way I’m feeling, and sometimes I just want everything to stop for a while.\n\nI’m not trying to be evasive. It’s just hard to separate out what counts as “hurting myself” versus being so tired and ashamed and stressed that I don’t know what to do with myself. I haven’t really talked about it much because it sounds dramatic when I say it out loud, and I don’t want people to overreact, but I also don’t want to pretend everything’s fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought that you would be better off dead?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure. I mean, I’ve had moments where I think it would be easier if I didn’t have to keep dealing with all of this, or if I could just disappear for a while and not have everyone needing an answer from me. But “better off dead” sounds so final, and I don’t know if that’s exactly what I mean.\n\nI don’t want to say no too quickly, because then it sounds like I’m brushing it off, and I don’t want to lie to you. But I also don’t want to make it sound like I’m sitting around clearly thinking, “I should be dead,” because that’s not how it feels in my head. It’s more muddled than that. Like I’m exhausted and ashamed and I just want out from under it for a minute.\n\nSo… I guess I don’t know. Maybe sometimes it gets close to that thought, but I don’t have a clear answer.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been about four months now, I think. It kind of started when the foreclosure notices began coming in. At first I told myself I was just stressed and that once I got through the next letter or phone call or whatever, I’d settle back down. But it didn’t really lift.\n\nSo… yeah, roughly four months. It’s hard to put an exact day on it, because it crept up on me, but those notices were when I remember feeling like something really shifted. Since then it’s just been hanging over me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss or gain since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I haven’t been tracking it or getting on a scale much. My clothes maybe feel a little different some days, but I don’t know if that’s actual weight change or just me not paying much attention to myself lately.\n\nI wouldn’t want to say I’ve lost or gained a significant amount because I don’t really have a number for you. If it’s happened, it hasn’t been obvious enough that I could point to it and say, yes, that’s definitely changed.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods in the last four months where you felt somewhat better, even if only for a few days?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it “better,” exactly. There have been a few days where I functioned a little more normally, I guess—like I got through the day and wasn’t actively thinking about it every second. But even then it’s kind of like background noise, if that makes sense. It’s still there, just not as loud for a little while.\n\nI can’t think of a stretch where I felt like myself again for several days in a row. Maybe part of a day here or there, or a day where I was distracted enough that I seemed okay on the outside. But then something would remind me, or I’d have a quiet moment, and it would all come right back.\n\nSo… somewhat better, maybe in small patches. Not really better in a way that lasted.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “pleasure” exactly. There are things I still do, but it feels more like I’m going through the motions than actually enjoying them.\n\nLike, sometimes I’ll put on a show or tinker with something around the house, and for a little bit I’m occupied, but I don’t get that same feeling of, I don’t know, being pulled into it or looking forward to it. It’s more like I’m trying to keep my hands busy so I don’t just sit there.\n\nI guess there are moments where something is mildly distracting or comforting, but I can’t think of anything lately where I’d say, yes, that actually made me happy the way it used to. It’s hard to tell if I’m not enjoying things at all, or if I’m just too preoccupied to notice.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past week where you felt no sadness at all?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had a whole day with no sadness at all. It’s hard to put an exact day on it, because it crept up on me, but those notices were when I remember feeling like something really shifted. Since then it’s just been hanging over me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss or gain since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I haven’t been tracking it or getting on a scale much. My clothes maybe feel a little different some days, but I don’t know if that’s actual weight change or just me not paying much attention to myself lately.\n\nI wouldn’t want to say I’ve lost or gained a significant amount because I don’t really have a number for you. If it’s happened, it hasn’t been obvious enough that I could point to it and say, yes, that’s definitely changed.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods in the last four months where you felt somewhat better, even if only for a few days?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it “better,” exactly. There have been a few days where I functioned a little more normally, I guess—like I got through the day and wasn’t actively thinking about it every second. But even then it’s kind of like background noise, if that makes sense. It’s still there, just not as loud for a little while.\n\nI can’t think of a stretch where I felt like myself again for several days in a row. Maybe part of a day here or there, or a day where I was distracted enough that I seemed okay on the outside. But then something would remind me, or I’d have a quiet moment, and it would all come right back.\n\nSo… somewhat better, maybe in small patches. Not really better in a way that lasted.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “pleasure” exactly. There are things I still do, but it feels more like I’m going through the motions than actually enjoying them.\n\nLike, sometimes I’ll put on a show or tinker with something around the house, and for a little bit I’m occupied, but I don’t get that same feeling of, I don’t know, being pulled into it or looking forward to it. It’s more like I’m trying to keep my hands busy so I don’t just sit there.\n\nI guess there are moments where something is mildly distracting or comforting, but I can’t think of anything lately where I’d say, yes, that actually made me happy the way it used to. It’s hard to tell if I’m not enjoying things at all, or if I’m just too preoccupied to notice.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past week where you felt no sadness at all?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had a whole day with no sadness at all. Maybe there are minutes where I’m distracted, like if I’m focused on a repair or just trying to get through something, but it’s still kind of sitting there underneath everything.\n\nEver since those foreclosure notices started coming, I just feel like I’m coming apart at the seams. I’ll be in my truck before work crying and trying to pull myself together so nobody sees it. Then the phone rings and I jump, because I’m scared it’s going to be more bad news. Even at work, where I’m usually steady, I’ve been snapping at the apprentices and making stupid mistakes in the repair bay that I would never normally make.\n\nSo no, not a day with no sadness. It may get pushed to the side for a little while, but it doesn’t really leave.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last four months where you felt completely back to your normal self?\n\nPatient: No, I really don’t think so. Not completely. This has been going on for about four months now, pretty much since the foreclosure notices started coming in, and I can’t point to any time in there where I felt like, “Okay, I’m back to me.”\n\nThere might have been little stretches where I acted more normal, or where I was busy enough that I wasn’t focused on it every second, but that’s different. It’s not the same as actually feeling like myself again. Since all of that started, it’s just been… hanging over everything. So no, I haven’t had a period where I felt completely back to normal.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find you are unable to take pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there are several, honestly. I used to be a lot more active with things outside of work, and now I just can’t seem to get anything out of them, or I don’t even make myself go.\n\nChurch volunteering was a big one. I used to show up pretty regularly and help out, and it made me feel useful, like I was connected to people. Lately I’ve just stopped going. Same with my fitness classes — I used to feel better afterward, even if I had to drag myself there, but now the idea of being around everyone and going through the routine just feels pointless.\n\nAnd I used to go to truck-club outings, too. That was something I actually looked forward to before, talking shop and seeing what people were working on. Now I either don’t go, or if I think about going, it feels like I’d just be standing there pretending.\n\nEven at work it’s affected things. I’ve been taken out of the high-risk repair bay, which… that’s hard to admit, because I used to handle that kind of work. But I’m not where I need to be, and they noticed. So yes, there are definitely things I used to enjoy or take pride in that I just don’t feel able to take pleasure in right now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yes. Definitely more than two weeks. It’s been… it’s been long enough that it’s changed my routine, not just a bad week or two where I didn’t feel like going out.

I used to be pretty consistent with church volunteering, my fitness classes, and the truck-club outings. Those were things I actually kept up with, even when I was tired, because they mattered to me or at least gave me something to look forward to. And now I’ve just stopped showing up. I’ll think about going, and then I just don’t. Or I’ll tell myself I’ll go next time, and then next time comes and I still don’t.

And at work it’s noticeable too. I’ve been taken out of the high-risk repair bay, which is hard to say because that was something I used to handle and take pride in. So yes, it’s been a significant loss of interest, and it’s gone on well past two weeks.

Some nights I’m just lying there thinking too much, and then other nights I’m so worn out I don’t even know when I fell asleep. It’s hard for me to tell what counts as “trouble sleeping” versus just being exhausted and stressed, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I used to be pretty good about having things outside of work, you know? Church volunteering, my fitness classes, and I’m in this truck club where we’d do outings and meetups, and normally I’d make time for all of that even when I was tired.\n\nBut lately I’ve just stopped showing up. I’ll tell myself I’m going to go, and then when it’s time, I just… don’t. It feels like too much, or I feel embarrassed because I don’t want people asking how I’m doing and then I have to pretend I’m fine. So I’ve kind of disappeared from all those things.\n\nAnd work has changed too. I got taken out of the high-risk repair bay, which… that one really stings, because that’s what I’m good at. Or was good at, I guess. I know it’s probably for safety, but it makes me feel like I’m not even trusted to do my own job right now. So, no, I’m not really enjoying much of anything I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Are you still able to find pleasure in the things you do?\n\nPatient: Not much, honestly. I keep thinking if I just push myself to do the normal things, maybe I’ll feel like myself again, but I don’t really get that lift from them anymore.\n\nLike with church volunteering—I used to feel useful there, like I was part of something steady. And my fitness classes, even when I complained about going, I always felt better afterward. The truck-club outings too, those were fun for me, getting out and talking with people who understood that whole world. But lately I’ve just stopped showing up. I’ll make excuses or just not answer messages because the thought of going feels like another job I can’t handle.\n\nAnd at work, being taken out of the high-risk repair bay… that’s been really hard. That was the part of the job where I felt competent, where I knew I was good. Now even work doesn’t give me that same sense of pride. It’s like the things that used to make me feel like me are still there, but I can’t really connect to them.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I don’t think I’ve been sitting there making a plan to hurt myself, if that’s what you mean. It’s more like… I get overwhelmed and I don’t want to feel the way I’m feeling, and sometimes I just want everything to stop for a while.\n\nI’m not trying to be evasive. It’s just hard to separate out what counts as “hurting myself” versus being so tired and ashamed and stressed that I don’t know what to do with myself. I haven’t really talked about it much because it sounds dramatic when I say it out loud, and I don’t want people to overreact, but I also don’t want to pretend everything’s fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought that you would be better off dead?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure. I mean, I’ve had moments where I think it would be easier if I didn’t have to keep dealing with all of this, or if I could just disappear for a while and not have everyone needing an answer from me. But “better off dead” sounds so final, and I don’t know if that’s exactly what I mean.\n\nI don’t want to say no too quickly, because then it sounds like I’m brushing it off, and I don’t want to lie to you. But I also don’t want to make it sound like I’m sitting around clearly thinking, “I should be dead,” because that’s not how it feels in my head. It’s more muddled than that. Like I’m exhausted and ashamed and I just want out from under it for a minute.\n\nSo… I guess I don’t know. Maybe sometimes it gets close to that thought, but I don’t have a clear answer.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been about four months now, I think. It kind of started when the foreclosure notices began coming in. At first I told myself I was just stressed and that once I got through the next letter or phone call or whatever, I’d settle back down. But it didn’t really lift.\n\nSo… yeah, roughly four months. It’s hard to put an exact day on it, because it crept up on me, but those notices were when I remember feeling like something really shifted. Since then it’s just been hanging over me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss or gain since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I haven’t been tracking it or getting on a scale much. My clothes maybe feel a little different some days, but I don’t know if that’s actual weight change or just me not paying much attention to myself lately.\n\nI wouldn’t want to say I’ve lost or gained a significant amount because I don’t really have a number for you. If it’s happened, it hasn’t been obvious enough that I could point to it and say, yes, that’s definitely changed.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods in the last four months where you felt somewhat better, even if only for a few days?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it “better,” exactly. There have been a few days where I functioned a little more normally, I guess—like I got through the day and wasn’t actively thinking about it every second. But even then it’s kind of like background noise, if that makes sense. It’s still there, just not as loud for a little while.\n\nI can’t think of a stretch where I felt like myself again for several days in a row. Maybe part of a day here or there, or a day where I was distracted enough that I seemed okay on the outside. But then something would remind me, or I’d have a quiet moment, and it would all come right back.\n\nSo… somewhat better, maybe in small patches. Not really better in a way that lasted.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “pleasure” exactly. There are things I still do, but it feels more like I’m going through the motions than actually enjoying them.\n\nLike, sometimes I’ll put on a show or tinker with something around the house, and for a little bit I’m occupied, but I don’t get that same feeling of, I don’t know, being pulled into it or looking forward to it. It’s more like I’m trying to keep my hands busy so I don’t just sit there.\n\nI guess there are moments where something is mildly distracting or comforting, but I can’t think of anything lately where I’d say, yes, that actually made me happy the way it used to. It’s hard to tell if I’m not enjoying things at all, or if I’m just too preoccupied to notice.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past week where you felt no sadness at all?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had a whole day with no sadness at all. Maybe there are minutes where I’m distracted, like if I’m focused on a repair or just trying to get through something, but it’s still kind of sitting there underneath everything.\n\nEver since those foreclosure notices started coming, I just feel like I’m coming apart at the seams. I’ll be in my truck before work crying and trying to pull myself together so nobody sees it. Then the phone rings and I jump, because I’m scared it’s going to be more bad news. Even at work, where I’m usually steady, I’ve been snapping at the apprentices and making stupid mistakes in the repair bay that I would never normally make.\n\nSo no, not a day with no sadness. It may get pushed to the side for a little while, but it doesn’t really leave.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last four months where you felt completely back to your normal self?\n\nPatient: No, I really don’t think so. Not completely. This has been going on for about four months now, pretty much since the foreclosure notices started coming in, and I can’t point to any time in there where I felt like, “Okay, I’m back to me.”\n\nThere might have been little stretches where I acted more normal, or where I was busy enough that I wasn’t focused on it every second, but that’s different. It’s not the same as actually feeling like myself again. Since all of that started, it’s just been… hanging over everything. So no, I haven’t had a period where I felt completely back to normal.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find you are unable to take pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there are several, honestly. I used to be a lot more active with things outside of work, and now I just can’t seem to get anything out of them, or I don’t even make myself go.\n\nChurch volunteering was a big one. I used to show up pretty regularly and help out, and it made me feel useful, like I was connected to people. Lately I’ve just stopped going. Same with my fitness classes — I used to feel better afterward, even if I had to drag myself there, but now the idea of being around everyone and going through the routine just feels pointless.\n\nAnd I used to go to truck-club outings, too. That was something I actually looked forward to before, talking shop and seeing what people were working on. Now I either don’t go, or if I think about going, it feels like I’d just be standing there pretending.\n\nEven at work it’s affected things. I’ve been taken out of the high-risk repair bay, which… that’s hard to admit, because I used to handle that kind of work. But I’m not where I need to be, and they noticed. So yes, there are definitely things I used to enjoy or take pride in that I just don’t feel able to take pleasure in right now.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m honestly not sure. I wish I had a better answer, like some one thing I can point to and say, “Yes, that still feels good,” but I don’t really know.\n\nThere are moments where I’m distracted, I guess, or where something doesn’t feel as heavy for a few minutes. But I don’t know if I’d call that pleasure exactly. It’s more like a break in the noise, not really enjoying something the way I used to.\n\nI’ve been trying to think about it, because I know there probably should be something, even small, but nothing really comes to mind right now. Maybe if I’m doing something with my hands and I’m focused, there’s a little bit of relief in that, but it’s not the same as feeling happy or actually looking forward to it. So… I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed much that I’d say I still take pleasure in.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant loss of interest in activities that you normally enjoy for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes. Definitely more than two weeks. It’s been… it’s been long enough that it’s changed my routine, not just a bad week or two where I didn’t feel like going out.\n\nI used to be pretty consistent with church volunteering, my fitness classes, and the truck-club outings. Those were things I actually kept up with, even when I was tired, because they mattered to me or at least gave me something to look forward to. And now I’ve just stopped showing up. I’ll think about going, and then I just don’t. Or I’ll tell myself I’ll go next time, and then next time comes and I still don’t.\n\nAnd at work it’s noticeable too. I’ve been taken out of the high-risk repair bay, which is hard to say because that was something I used to handle and take pride in. So yes, it’s been a significant loss of interest, and it’s gone on well past two weeks.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

Maybe if I’m doing something with my hands and I’m focused, there’s a little bit of relief in that, but it’s not the same as feeling happy or actually looking forward to it. So… I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed much that I’d say I still take pleasure in.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant loss of interest in activities that you normally enjoy for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes. Definitely more than two weeks. It’s been… it’s been long enough that it’s changed my routine, not just a bad week or two where I didn’t feel like going out.\n\nI used to be pretty consistent with church volunteering, my fitness classes, and the truck-club outings. Those were things I actually kept up with, even when I was tired, because they mattered to me or at least gave me something to look forward to. And now I’ve just stopped showing up. I’ll think about going, and then I just don’t. Or I’ll tell myself I’ll go next time, and then next time comes and I still don’t.\n\nAnd at work it’s noticeable too. I’ve been taken out of the high-risk repair bay, which is hard to say because that was something I used to handle and take pride in. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think so. I mean, “almost all” sounds like a big thing to say, but when I look at how my days actually go, it feels pretty close to that.

It’s not just that I stopped one hobby or skipped a couple things because I was busy. I’ve stopped showing up for church volunteering, and that used to be something I kept up with because it made me feel connected and useful. I’ve stopped going to my fitness classes, too, even though those were part of my routine. And the truck-club outings… I used to enjoy those, really enjoy them, and now I just don’t go.

So when you ask if it’s most of the day, I’d say yes — it kind of sits over everything. I’m not moving toward things the way I used to, even things that used to matter to me.

Like things I know better than to miss. It’s embarrassing, and it scares me, because I’ve always been the person who keeps it together. Lately it feels like I’m coming apart at the seams.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I know my sleep hasn’t felt normal, but I haven’t really been keeping track of it or anything. Some nights I’m just lying there thinking too much, and then other nights I’m so worn out I don’t even know when I fell asleep. It’s hard for me to tell what counts as “trouble sleeping” versus just being exhausted and stressed, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I used to be pretty good about having things outside of work, you know? Church volunteering, my fitness classes, and I’m in this truck club where we’d do outings and meetups, and normally I’d make time for all of that even when I was tired.\n\nBut lately I’ve just stopped showing up. I’ll tell myself I’m going to go, and then when it’s time, I just… don’t. It feels like too much, or I feel embarrassed because I don’t want people asking how I’m doing and then I have to pretend I’m fine. So I’ve kind of disappeared from all those things.\n\nAnd work has changed too. I got taken out of the high-risk repair bay, which… that one really stings, because that’s what I’m good at. Or was good at, I guess. I know it’s probably for safety, but it makes me feel like I’m not even trusted to do my own job right now. So, no, I’m not really enjoying much of anything I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Are you still able to find pleasure in the things you do?\n\nPatient: Not much, honestly. I keep thinking if I just push myself to do the normal things, maybe I’ll feel like myself again, but I don’t really get that lift from them anymore.\n\nLike with church volunteering—I used to feel useful there, like I was part of something steady. And my fitness classes, even when I complained about going, I always felt better afterward. The truck-club outings too, those were fun for me, getting out and talking with people who understood that whole world. But lately I’ve just stopped showing up. I’ll make excuses or just not answer messages because the thought of going feels like another job I can’t handle.\n\nAnd at work, being taken out of the high-risk repair bay… that’s been really hard. That was the part of the job where I felt competent, where I knew I was good. Now even work doesn’t give me that same sense of pride. It’s like the things that used to make me feel like me are still there, but I can’t really connect to them.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I don’t think I’ve been sitting there making a plan to hurt myself, if that’s what you mean. It’s more like… I get overwhelmed and I don’t want to feel the way I’m feeling, and sometimes I just want everything to stop for a while.\n\nI’m not trying to be evasive. It’s just hard to separate out what counts as “hurting myself” versus being so tired and ashamed and stressed that I don’t know what to do with myself. I haven’t really talked about it much because it sounds dramatic when I say it out loud, and I don’t want people to overreact, but I also don’t want to pretend everything’s fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought that you would be better off dead?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure. I mean, I’ve had moments where I think it would be easier if I didn’t have to keep dealing with all of this, or if I could just disappear for a while and not have everyone needing an answer from me. But “better off dead” sounds so final, and I don’t know if that’s exactly what I mean.\n\nI don’t want to say no too quickly, because then it sounds like I’m brushing it off, and I don’t want to lie to you. But I also don’t want to make it sound like I’m sitting around clearly thinking, “I should be dead,” because that’s not how it feels in my head. It’s more muddled than that. Like I’m exhausted and ashamed and I just want out from under it for a minute.\n\nSo… I guess I don’t know. Maybe sometimes it gets close to that thought, but I don’t have a clear answer.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been about four months now, I think. It kind of started when the foreclosure notices began coming in. At first I told myself I was just stressed and that once I got through the next letter or phone call or whatever, I’d settle back down. But it didn’t really lift.\n\nSo… yeah, roughly four months. It’s hard to put an exact day on it, because it crept up on me, but those notices were when I remember feeling like something really shifted. Since then it’s just been hanging over me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss or gain since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I haven’t been tracking it or getting on a scale much. My clothes maybe feel a little different some days, but I don’t know if that’s actual weight change or just me not paying much attention to myself lately.\n\nI wouldn’t want to say I’ve lost or gained a significant amount because I don’t really have a number for you. If it’s happened, it hasn’t been obvious enough that I could point to it and say, yes, that’s definitely changed.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods in the last four months where you felt somewhat better, even if only for a few days?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it “better,” exactly. There have been a few days where I functioned a little more normally, I guess—like I got through the day and wasn’t actively thinking about it every second. But even then it’s kind of like background noise, if that makes sense. It’s still there, just not as loud for a little while.\n\nI can’t think of a stretch where I felt like myself again for several days in a row. Maybe part of a day here or there, or a day where I was distracted enough that I seemed okay on the outside. But then something would remind me, or I’d have a quiet moment, and it would all come right back.\n\nSo… somewhat better, maybe in small patches. Not really better in a way that lasted.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “pleasure” exactly. It’s more like I’m trying to keep my hands busy so I don’t just sit there.\n\nI guess there are moments where something is mildly distracting or comforting, but I can’t think of anything lately where I’d say, yes, that actually made me happy the way it used to. It’s hard to tell if I’m not enjoying things at all, or if I’m just too preoccupied to notice.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past week where you felt no sadness at all?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had a whole day with no sadness at all. Maybe there are minutes where I’m distracted, like if I’m focused on a repair or just trying to get through something, but it’s still kind of sitting there underneath everything.\n\nEver since those foreclosure notices started coming, I just feel like I’m coming apart at the seams. I’ll be in my truck before work crying and trying to pull myself together so nobody sees it. Then the phone rings and I jump, because I’m scared it’s going to be more bad news. Even at work, where I’m usually steady, I’ve been snapping at the apprentices and making stupid mistakes in the repair bay that I would never normally make.\n\nSo no, not a day with no sadness. I mean, I’ve had moments where I think it would be easier if I didn’t have to keep dealing with all of this, or if I could just disappear for a while and not have everyone needing an answer from me. But “better off dead” sounds so final, and I don’t know if that’s exactly what I mean.\n\nI don’t want to say no too quickly, because then it sounds like I’m brushing it off, and I don’t want to lie to you. But I also don’t want to make it sound like I’m sitting around clearly thinking, “I should be dead,” because that’s not how it feels in my head. It’s more muddled than that. Like I’m exhausted and ashamed and I just want out from under it for a minute.\n\nSo… I guess I don’t know. Maybe sometimes it gets close to that thought, but I don’t have a clear answer.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been about four months now, I think. It kind of started when the foreclosure notices began coming in. At first I told myself I was just stressed and that once I got through the next letter or phone call or whatever, I’d settle back down. But it didn’t really lift.\n\nSo… yeah, roughly four months. It’s hard to put an exact day on it, because it crept up on me, but those notices were when I remember feeling like something really shifted. Since then it’s just been hanging over me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss or gain since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I haven’t been tracking it or getting on a scale much. My clothes maybe feel a little different some days, but I don’t know if that’s actual weight change or just me not paying much attention to myself lately.\n\nI wouldn’t want to say I’ve lost or gained a significant amount because I don’t really have a number for you. If it’s happened, it hasn’t been obvious enough that I could point to it and say, yes, that’s definitely changed.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods in the last four months where you felt somewhat better, even if only for a few days?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it “better,” exactly. There have been a few days where I functioned a little more normally, I guess—like I got through the day and wasn’t actively thinking about it every second. But even then it’s kind of like background noise, if that makes sense. It’s still there, just not as loud for a little while.\n\nI can’t think of a stretch where I felt like myself again for several days in a row. Maybe part of a day here or there, or a day where I was distracted enough that I seemed okay on the outside. But then something would remind me, or I’d have a quiet moment, and it would all come right back.\n\nSo… somewhat better, maybe in small patches. Not really better in a way that lasted.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “pleasure” exactly. There are things I still do, but it feels more like I’m going through the motions than actually enjoying them.\n\nLike, sometimes I’ll put on a show or tinker with something around the house, and for a little bit I’m occupied, but I don’t get that same feeling of, I don’t know, being pulled into it or looking forward to it. It’s more like I’m trying to keep my hands busy so I don’t just sit there.\n\nI guess there are moments where something is mildly distracting or comforting, but I can’t think of anything lately where I’d say, yes, that actually made me happy the way it used to. It’s hard to tell if I’m not enjoying things at all, or if I’m just too preoccupied to notice.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past week where you felt no sadness at all?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had a whole day with no sadness at all. Maybe there are minutes where I’m distracted, like if I’m focused on a repair or just trying to get through something, but it’s still kind of sitting there underneath everything.\n\nEver since those foreclosure notices started coming, I just feel like I’m coming apart at the seams. I’ll be in my truck before work crying and trying to pull myself together so nobody sees it. Then the phone rings and I jump, because I’m scared it’s going to be more bad news. Even at work, where I’m usually steady, I’ve been snapping at the apprentices and making stupid mistakes in the repair bay that I would never normally make.\n\nSo no, not a day with no sadness. It may get pushed to the side for a little while, but it doesn’t really leave.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last four months where you felt completely back to your normal self?\n\nPatient: No, I really don’t think so. Not completely. This has been going on for about four months now, pretty much since the foreclosure notices started coming in, and I can’t point to any time in there where I felt like, “Okay, I’m back to me.”\n\nThere might have been little stretches where I acted more normal, or where I was busy enough that I wasn’t focused on it every second, but that’s different. It’s not the same as actually feeling like myself again. Since all of that started, it’s just been… hanging over everything. So no, I haven’t had a period where I felt completely back to normal.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find you are unable to take pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there are several, honestly. I used to be a lot more active with things outside of work, and now I just can’t seem to get anything out of them, or I don’t even make myself go.\n\nChurch volunteering was a big one. I used to show up pretty regularly and help out, and it made me feel useful, like I was connected to people. Lately I’ve just stopped going. Same with my fitness classes — I used to feel better afterward, even if I had to drag myself there, but now the idea of being around everyone and going through the routine just feels pointless.\n\nAnd I used to go to truck-club outings, too. That was something I actually looked forward to before, talking shop and seeing what people were working on. Now I either don’t go, or if I think about going, it feels like I’d just be standing there pretending.\n\nEven at work it’s affected things. I’ve been taken out of the high-risk repair bay, which… that’s hard to admit, because I used to handle that kind of work. But I’m not where I need to be, and they noticed. So yes, there are definitely things I used to enjoy or take pride in that I just don’t feel able to take pleasure in right now.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m honestly not sure. I wish I had a better answer, like some one thing I can point to and say, “Yes, that still feels good,” but I don’t really know.\n\nThere are moments where I’m distracted, I guess, or where something doesn’t feel as heavy for a few minutes. But I don’t know if I’d call that pleasure exactly. It’s more like a break in the noise, not really enjoying something the way I used to.\n\nI’ve been trying to think about it, because I know there probably should be something, even small, but nothing really comes to mind right now. Maybe if I’m doing something with my hands and I’m focused, there’s a little bit of relief in that, but it’s not the same as feeling happy or actually looking forward to it. So… I don’t know. I haven’t really noticed much that I’d say I still take pleasure in.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant loss of interest in activities that you normally enjoy for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes. Definitely more than two weeks. It’s been… it’s been long enough that it’s changed my routine, not just a bad week or two where I didn’t feel like going out.\n\nI used to be pretty consistent with church volunteering, my fitness classes, and the truck-club outings. Those were things I actually kept up with, even when I was tired, because they mattered to me or at least gave me something to look forward to. And now I’ve just stopped showing up. I’ll think about going, and then I just don’t. Or I’ll tell myself I’ll go next time, and then next time comes and I still don’t.\n\nAnd at work it’s noticeable too. I’ve been taken out of the high-risk repair bay, which is hard to say because that was something I used to handle and take pride in. So yes, it’s been a significant loss of interest, and it’s gone on well past two weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a lack of interest in almost all activities for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, “almost all” sounds like a big thing to say, but when I look at how my days actually go, it feels pretty close to that.\n\nIt’s not just that I stopped one hobby or skipped a couple things because I was busy.
